If a step fails while writing, what that publish wrote is undone at once, last write first; **Carry on** picks up one that stopped, and **Roll back** undoes the whole publish for 14 days (a row live changed since is left as live has it and listed). It can also be **Scheduled** inside the release window: the preview is worked out again at the time, and it stops if a new conflict has appeared. Until **Keep staging caught up** is switched on, Publish carries uploaded zips only.
### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I change what a page says?
First switch on **Edit site**: the pencil-in-a-square icon in the bar at the very top, beside your dashboard (⌂) and sign-out icons. It lights up while on, stays on for that device until you tap it again, and while it's off you see every page exactly as visitors do, published content only. With it on, press **Edit** on the part of the page that's outlined for you (staff with **Pages: edit**, the Founder until a role is given it in **System Configuration → Permissions**); drafts show there, marked Draft, for our team only. The first places are the home page, under the four doors, and every page of ours, under its text; more come as releases add them. A panel opens (from the bottom on a phone, at the side on a computer). **Add** holds the blocks: a heading, text (paragraphs, lists, **bold**, *italic*, links), a picture (uploaded here, kept with our pages), a button, a divider and a spacer. Tap one to add it, or drag it to its place on the page. **Layout** shows the sections (one, two or three columns each) and their blocks: drag ⠿ to move a block, between columns and sections too, or a whole section. Tap a block on the page to pick it: its toolbar (on a computer, over the block; on a phone, in the panel) moves it up or down, copies or removes it; **Manage** holds its words, **Design** its alignment, width and the space above and below. A section has its columns, background, inner space, column widths and order. The buttons at the top show the part as a **desktop**, **tablet** or **phone** sees it, and Design sets each one on its own: a tablet takes the desktop's settings unless you set it, a phone takes the tablet's (on a phone, columns always stack). Every change shows on the page at once, as visitors will see it. Under **Add → Parts of the site** are pieces of the site itself, which draw themselves from our data and stay current: a **search box** (empty, or with words already in it), **businesses** (cards from our directory for a town and, if you like, a kind of business, with a link to all of them), a **map button** (opens the site's map of those businesses), a **guide** (a card for a town's guide, shown once it has something published) and a **GO Code** (the QR code and trvgo.us address for this page or another of ours). You choose what each shows; how it works stays the site's. On one of our own pages, the **Page** tab sets its title, the description search engines show and the picture used when it's shared; these are saved for the page at once, apart from the draft. **Save draft** keeps it for our team only, right there on the page. **Publish** (staff with **Pages: publish**) puts it live for everyone as the next version; ▾ → **Versions** lists them, with who and when, and **Roll back** puts any earlier one live again, so nothing is lost. ▾ → **Publish later** picks a day and time instead: what's in the panel then goes live by itself as the next version, at the first visit to the page after that time, and your draft stays yours to keep working on (**Call it off** stops it). Two outlined places on the home page are on every page of the site: **Every page, under the header** and **Every page, above the footer**. What's published there shows on all our browsing pages (not on the admin, sign-in or payment pages), for notices like holiday hours or an event; the header's and footer's own menus and links stay as they are. If a published version has nothing in it, the outlined part says so and points to Roll back. What the page showed before stays until something is published there. On our own pages, Add → Parts of the site also has **Likes and comments** (the page's views, likes, Share and QR code, with its public comments or the bar alone) and **Form** (a contact form or a quote request; messages come to Marketplace & Storefronts → Form entries, as with a page's own form). They're offered only on our pages, where there's a page to like and to send the message about.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Why don't I see the editing outlines and drafts any more?
Editing pages is switched off until you turn it on (Jason, 2026-11-18). Tap **Edit site**, the pencil-in-a-square icon in the bar at the very top, beside your dashboard (⌂) and sign-out icons; it lights up, stays on for that device, and the outlines, drafts and **Edit** buttons show again. Tap it again to see the page as visitors do. A business editing its own storefront doesn't need the switch.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I see what the crawler is doing?
In the admin, **Insights → Crawler**. The crawler is one service that the site's workflows use (finding businesses, keeping listings current, reading our sources, the heritage catalog, landmarks, how-to guides, search learning), each with its share of every run. Every 5 minutes the site plans the run: finding businesses first, then whoever has waited longest. Each workflow folds open to show its tasks: how often each runs, when it last did, what the last day's runs did with it (did its work, had nothing to do, wasn't reached, failed, or is off and why), what's waiting in its queue, and what it spent this month (Claude, web search, Google, counted even when a call fails). A task a run didn't reach is never counted as a failure: it goes first next time. From there, Operations staff can **Pause** or **Resume** a workflow, change its **share**, **Run first** a task, **Switch off** a task, and **Retry failed** work. Below them, **The last runs** shows each run task by task. A run that couldn't work shows as failed on Railway too, and Pulse opens a case when no run has started for 30 minutes or six in a row have failed. Queueing coverage gaps by hand is still on the earlier Crawler overview, linked there, until it moves.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I clean up bad data the crawler added?
In the admin, **Insights → Data cleanup** (Owners and Executives by default; **System Configuration → Permissions** can give it to others). It finds what the crawler made before today's rules and fails them: listings whose name is page text ("Contact Us:", "PO Box 190", a webinar or form title), listings with no address and no phone, listings resting only on pages we may only cite, values copied from OpenStreetMap alone, a "Closed" set on one site's word, likely duplicates (never providers at one clinic or businesses sharing a building), staged finds and place-store entries with page-text names, places the guards would hold, and landmarks whose names mark a sacred, burial or archaeological place, or one specific to a nation, shown too openly, or holding Wikipedia's words. Choose one rule or every rule: **Preview** shows how many would change, how many need a person (**Check these**) and how many it leaves, with examples you can open, and changes nothing. **Run a pass** acts by rule: a listing that fails is taken off the public site and goes back to work (a "Help us find this business" request, or a sighting back in Review) or, when nothing in it can be used, is listed under **Restricted** (see below), OpenStreetMap-only values are marked not yet confirmed, a one-site Closed becomes a caution with "Still open?", likely duplicates go to the identity recheck (see "How do you keep a business from being listed twice?"), place-store entries and landmarks are held, and a landmark's Wikipedia text is cleared only once our own description of it is written. Up to 600 records a press (**Continue** carries on). Every change is listed under **Passes** with **Undo** for one record or the whole pass, and nothing is ever deleted. What a pass left as it is shows there too, with why (waiting for its own description, already asked about in Review, over the cap of open merge offers, its own website says it closed). A daily pass is there too, switched off, and it runs only while automation is on.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Where do the listings with no address or phone go?
Data cleanup takes a listing with a name and a town but no address or phone off the site and keeps it as a **Help us find this business** request: shown on its town's page and in search, asking visitors for its phone or address and its owner to claim it. A visitor's answer arrives in Review as a suggestion with how they know; accept the phone or address there and it's a listing again by itself (the owner adding one after claiming does the same). **Insights → Data cleanup** shows how many are being asked about, found, and not shown (a person's or a protected name, or no answer in 180 days). Drop boxes and collection boxes (USPS, FedEx, UPS) are no longer treated as page text: they're real places people use. They're their own kind of listing now, **Mail & package drop-off**, with their map pins; a pass for "Mail & package drop-offs" sorts them, and puts back the ones an earlier pass took off. They can't be claimed and stay out of business browse lists. **Drop-offs without a map pin** places those with a street address from the U.S. Census (free, 25 a press); the rest get a pin dropped in Edit listing. The crawler's own check for page-text names is now the same as Data cleanup's, so new page text is held for a person instead of going live.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What happens to the records Data cleanup takes off?
Nothing is left hidden with nowhere to go. A record a pass takes off goes back to work, or is listed as restricted. **Back to work:** a name and a town becomes a "Help us find this business" request; a name only, or a phone and address that rest only on pages we may only cite, goes back to Review as a sighting; page text for a name, but with a phone or a street address, goes back as a sighting "name unknown", its phone and address kept while its real name is looked for. Sightings are looked up again on the retry ladder (a day, then 7, 30 and 180 days, and on for good; anything new starts it over), and an answer from a source we may keep shows on the sighting in Review. Merge it into its hidden listing there and it's a listing again; approve it as another listing and the hidden one points to it; turn it down and it's restricted, with that reason. **Restricted** (kept for our records only, never shown): page text with nothing else, staged finds and place-store names that are page text, facts from pages we may only cite (their links kept as citations), and landmarks held for their names (shown as a story only once landmarks have that level, and a nation's places only as the nation says). **Insights → Data cleanup → Restricted** lists every one with its reason, **Put back** (undoes the cleanup's change) and **Make it a request** (sends it back to work; greyed with the reason where it can't, such as a nation's places). Records an earlier pass took off are sorted the same way by a pass for "Taken off by an earlier pass".

### I'm on the Traversence team. Will Data cleanup change something a person entered?
No. What an owner, our team or a member entered, a claimed listing's owner's fields, and anything a person already decided (a find approved in the intake, a field someone set, a landmark someone approved) is never changed by it. Those are counted under **Check these** and gathered in one Pulse case, "Data cleanup needs a person", a step for each kind with **Looked at them**, alongside the merge offers waiting in Review. **Undo** never overwrites a later change either: anything someone changed after the pass is left as it is now.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I pause the crawler and everything else that gathers or creates content?
In the admin, **System Configuration → Automation** (Owners and Executives switch; other staff can look). The **master switch** pauses everything that gathers or creates content at once: the crawler's runs on Railway end as soon as they start (even if Railway's schedule comes back), the site's own workflow jobs skip (Find it and guide drafts, guides on demand, suggested journeys, the heritage catalog, the listings pipeline, landmark photos), and the AI features and outside calls that serve them wait. It never stops business operations: orders, billing and payments, email, releases and staging, Moments, members' and businesses' own actions (a card scan, Write it for me), the admin itself, and guides' weather and roads. Below it you can pause one **workflow** (W0–W14; W0 is the indexer, which Start the system switches on first), switch off one crawler **task**, or pause one outside **service**. Pausing asks why; each switch shows its reason, who set it and when, and the page keeps a list of the changes. A paused thing shows as paused, never as a failure, and raises no warning in Pulse. Since 2026-11-14 the master switch is off ("Paused by Jason, 2026-11-14, until the crawler review"), so nothing gathers until someone switches it on.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What are workflows, tasks, routes and services on the Automation page?
Four layers, and a call happens only when every one of them is on. A **workflow** is a job of work start to finish, such as Listings (W1) or Guides on demand (W5). A **task** is one piece of a workflow's work: one of the crawler's capture tasks, a scheduled job on the site, or an AI feature. A **route** is a task's use of one outside service: AI use sends each feature to its providers in order (a menu to Google Document AI, then AWS, then Claude), and the crawler's coverage task asks OpenStreetMap and Wikidata. A **service** is the company or registry called, with its card in Outside services. Pause a service and every route to it closes: the crawler holds its addresses (a task that needs it reports "held"), the site's callers skip it, and an AI feature routed to it waits or takes its next provider. Services that business operations depend on (billing, payments, email, files, sign-in, the site itself, guides' weather and roads) aren't paused from there, and each says why. The ⓘ on the page explains it too.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Where are the crawler's workflows and the Workflow board?
On **System Configuration → Workflows** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/workflows#crawler), under the admin's own workflows (orders' stages and moves, unchanged). Since the control board's build each crawler workflow, **W0 to W14**, is a gauge card: its stage, its score (how much of its setup is done until it's watched, then its reading against its target) and its state in words ("Paused by the system (a trip): …" for a trip, never "paused by a person"). **W13 Stories** is a list (it's made of other workflows' work) and **W14 Authority sources** says **Not built** until it is. Each card opens the workflow's own page in the side panel: its last run, what returns empty, its steps with their switches and sources, its settings (each save a version, with Go back to it) and this month's spend; **By workflow, this month** is under the cards. **Paused now** at the top groups what's paused (Needs attention, Waiting on it, Off on purpose), each count a filter. **Start the system** and **Check before switching on** are on the System Control Board's **Start-up** stage now (https://traversence.com/admin/config/control?tab=startup), one home for each; the page links there. https://traversence.com/admin/config/board still opens this page.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Which account does each outside service sign in as?
Each service's card in the Service register (https://traversence.com/admin/config/register) says it on its top line: **Signs in as** and the account's email (for Google, its project too). Its portal links then open in that account where the company allows it (Google's Console, Credentials and Billing take it), and say which account to use where it doesn't. Not recorded yet? The card says so, with **Record it**: one field for the email. Once recorded, its setup shows one line, "Signs in as … · recorded by …" with the time, and a pencil to **Edit** it (the field, reopened in place); the value before it stays under **History**. The form goes away and the notice just says "Recorded." A record is edited, never undone. Each portal link is an icon; hover it (or press and hold on a phone) to see where it opens and as which account An email isn't a secret; passwords stay in the Vault (2026-11-23).

### I'm on the Traversence team. Where do I see every outside service we use?
In the admin, **System Configuration → Service register** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/register): one card for every outside service the site and the crawler depend on (since 2026-11-19 it holds what Outside services showed, and the old address opens the same card), the paid ones and the free ones (OpenStreetMap, Wikipedia and Wikidata, the National Weather Service, AZ 511, Recreation.gov, the NPI Registry, the IRS, the Library of Congress, the Census, Common Crawl and more). Each card says what we use it for, whether it's working (from the site's and the crawler's own logs, and anything known, like a service refusing us), what it costs ($0 for the free ones, with their usage rules), its terms and who to ask when something's wrong (its support or its community forum). What's set in another section (its licence on Sources, the crawler's tasks that call it, its scheduled tasks and switches) shows on the card as it stands, with a link there to change it. To find a card, press **Ctrl K** (or the search at the top) and type its name, its company or its address, like "overpass" or "weather.gov": it opens that card, and its linked sections show just under it. Each card also shows the crawler's own calls to that service: how many in the last 24 hours and this month, how many failed, and the last error (like an address that answers "406 Not Acceptable"). The site's own calls aren't counted there yet, but a service refusing the site shows there as an issue. When a crawler task that uses the service is switched off, its line says so first ("Switched off: nothing runs it until it's switched on") with **Switch on** right there, and a failure from before it was switched off shows as its last run, in plain words (a server's error page by its name, like "503 Service Unavailable"), never as the reason it isn't running (2026-11-19).

### I'm on the Traversence team. What does a service's stage mean, and how do I pause one?
Every outside service on **System Configuration → Service register** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/register) has a stage, shown in its row and first on its card, and the list filters by it. **Added**: a release brought it in and it waits for its key (one that came in switched off also waits for a person to activate it). **Key in**: its key is in place, and it waits for **Activate**. **Active**: its workflows can call it, inside its caps and paces. **Paused**: nothing of ours calls it until it's activated again; its key stays. **Retired**: kept as a record of why it stopped and what replaced it; the first is Overpass, OpenStreetMap's live query server, retired on 2026-11-19 at its community's request and replaced by our own copy from Geofabrik (OpenStreetMap itself stays active). **Pause** (with why) and **Activate** are on the service's card and on its row on the System Control Board; Activate runs the switch-on check first and stays paused, saying why, if a line is red. Each change is kept on the card under **Its stages**, with who, when and why. Services whose calls are business operations (Bluehost, payments, email and the like) aren't paused from here, and the card says so. A service's key is entered on its own card (see *How do I add or change a service's key?*). **Retire it…** (folded on the card, with why and what replaced it) stops everything of ours calling it and keeps its card, record and data; its row says **Retired**, nothing can switch it on until **Bring it back** (which leaves it paused until it's activated, through its check), and the Retired filter lists them. Under the list, **Proposed** is where you name a service you'd like added (its name, its site and what it would be for): a build request only, with no address, key or call until a release adds it; **Drop it**, and **Propose again**. **Removed** keeps the record of a service whose code and key are gone (none yet). Each service is defined in one place in the site's code, so a new one is added in one go (decisions/0173).

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I add or change a service's key?
On the service's card in **System Configuration → Service register** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/register), under **Its key**: each key is named, never shown, with whether it's in place. **Add its key** (or **Replace**) takes it in a hidden box and writes it into the site's settings for you, no cPanel trip, then runs the service's **Check** at once: a pass is the end of it; a fail says what the service answered, and **Put the old one back** undoes it (the old key is kept until a Check passes or you **Forget the old one**). **Remove the key** takes it out, kept the same way. Only the Founder can do these, never on staging, and the site's log records who changed which key and when, never the key itself. The Park Service's key (NPS_API_KEY, free from https://api.data.gov/signup/) is optional: it's for the Park Service's photos, and its records site needs none. Keys the crawler keeps on Railway (Google Places, Tavily, Brave, Recreation.gov, the Hugging Face token) are set there: the card links to the crawler's Variables on Railway, and **Check** confirms it once the crawler reports it has the key (never its value); Railway restarts the crawler when a variable changes.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do we keep track of a request we sent an outside company?
On the company's card in **System Configuration → Service register**, under **Waiting on them**, press **Note a request we sent** and give what you asked, its ticket number and a link. Each open request is a case in **Pulse** until you record their answer, with **Chase them** after 5 days. Requests have their own record now, so they're always kept; Name.com's reseller pricing ticket #3113488 is there.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Why does a service say "Off until a person switches it on"?
It was added switched off and waits for a person to switch it on, on **System Configuration → Automation** or the **System Control Board**. Its note says why it waits: the Arizona Corporation Commission and New Mexico's Secretary of State wait on our records requests, and the Arizona licence lists on Cloudflare being set up by hand.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Why does the Stripe or Square card say keys are missing?
The card names the exact settings it can't find. **Live** uses the plain set (for Stripe: STRIPE_CLIENT_ID, STRIPE_SECRET_KEY, STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY and STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET) with MARKET_PAY_ENV=live. Test accounts always pay in test mode with the test set (STRIPE_TEST_… or SQUARE_SANDBOX_…), and the card says when that set is missing too.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What happens when an outside service refuses us or stops answering?
The site and the crawler notice it, stop asking, and tell you, while everything that doesn't need that service carries on. Each failure is sorted by what it is: refused us (a block, like "406 Not Acceptable"), our key refused, over its quota, asked us to slow down, didn't answer, a server error, or an answer we can't read, and kept with its evidence (what the server said, when, and whether it was the crawler on Railway or the site). A block, a refused key or a used-up quota stops every call to that server until someone marks it resolved, or, for other services, until its light check says it's lifted; OpenStreetMap's servers get no contact at all while they refuse us, not even their status page, until a person marks them resolved; we never change our name to get round a block. When a server asks us to slow down (a 429, or a 503 that says so, as Common Crawl's data server's "SlowDown: Please reduce your request rate" does), we wait twice as long as for other failures, longer each time (or exactly as long as it says), so we ask that server half as often; it's a warning that clears itself when the server answers, never a trip, however long it lasts. A service's card says where its wait stands now (2026-11-20): "Backing off until …" while it waits, "Its wait ended …: the next call tries it again" once it's over, and "Paused by the system (a trip): no calls until its check passes" when a trip holds it, never a date that's passed or one far off. While it waits it doesn't hold what needs that service: only a refusal, a refused key, a used-up quota, or a wait that trips at its limit (6 in a row or 6 hours) holds a workflow's switch-on check. Its **Check it now** is greyed, saying when the next try is: pressing it would ask that server again straight after it asked us to slow down. Timeouts and server errors wait a little and try again by themselves, longer each time; after 6 in a row, or 6 hours, it's a trip on the Control Board, and calls to that server wait for an admin's **Resolve**. Each issue that needs a person raises one alarm on the **System Control Board** (and so in **Pulse**) naming the exact issue and the evidence, with **Check it now** (or, once it has tripped, **Check and start again**) and the service's card a press away; a passing check is what closes it (2026-11-19: no Mark resolved); it also shows on the service's card in **System Configuration → Outside services**. OpenStreetMap's Overpass servers are retired (2026-11-19): we no longer ask them at all, so there's no alarm for them; anything open about them closed as "Retired: we no longer use Overpass". A crawler task whose service is held says "Held", never "Failed", and the crawler's other work runs as usual. That holds for the call that trips the hold too, so a crawler run whose only trouble is a held service ends ok on Railway, never "Crashed". OpenStreetMap's main server (overpass-api.de) has refused our crawler since November 13: it stays on the list but isn't asked, or checked, while it refuses us. When OpenStreetMap tells us we may, a person presses Check and start again; it then comes back at half our budget for two weeks. Each service's servers are listed on its card, where the team can add one or pause one and see why one is held. The crawler now asks the other servers for one kind of place at a time, in small queries, one after another, and doesn't ask again for the same town and kind for three months. First, though, it reads our own copy of OpenStreetMap: Arizona's and New Mexico's map files, downloaded each week from Geofabrik (a free mirror), hold the kinds of place it looks for in a town (places to eat and stay, things to do, shops, services, groceries and gas, places of worship, campgrounds and most health care), so it asks the servers only for what our copy doesn't hold (a kind the files don't carry, or a town in another state). While every server refuses us, it carries on from our copy, and what it can't find there waits for a server, nothing lost. Those places are still OpenStreetMap's: a lead and a link, checked on each business's own website like any other. Web searches work the same way: when Tavily fails or is held, Brave answers instead, and the switch is logged. A paid service stands in for a free one only when a director switches it on, for one need, with its own monthly cap (**System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**, off until then): Google Places to confirm a business when its own website, the registries and both searches found nothing (only Google's map link is kept, never its details; a match raises the business's confidence, but never publishes a new find on its own), and Claude to read pages into facts when Gemini's free allowance can't. Each use goes on the cost ledger, and the pre-flight shows it on that need's line. Our own use stays at a quarter of what OpenStreetMap's public servers allow (2,500 queries a day, the crawler and the site together, and half that while a server blocks us and for two weeks after); at most one query a minute, spread over the day; Google checks and web searches are spread evenly through the day too, and when a service tells us to wait, we wait exactly as long as it says. The team sets these numbers in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**. Our requests carry our address (support@traversence.com) so the services we use can reach us.

### Why doesn't Traversence ask OpenStreetMap's servers any more?
Because OpenStreetMap's community asked us not to. Its public Overpass servers are run by volunteers to help people who edit the map, not as a back end for websites and apps; their answer to our statement (https://community.openstreetmap.org/t/150369) was that an app's fair share is about 50 requests a day, and to stop using them altogether. So since 2026-11-19 we don't, from our site or our crawler, and nothing retries them. We read our own copy of OpenStreetMap instead: Geofabrik's free weekly files for Arizona, New Mexico, Utah, Colorado and Nevada (the last three, and health care, added the same day). What that means: places from OpenStreetMap show in those five states only, and a kind of place the copy doesn't carry comes from our other sources. In the admin, a crawler run that met one says so on W1's row of the System Control Board ("some places aren't in our OpenStreetMap copy"), and OpenStreetMap's card in **System Configuration → Service register** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/register#svc-osm) lists its servers as retired.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What is the System Control Board?
The control room for the whole system. Clicking **System Configuration** in the side panel opens it.

- **How it opens** (2026-11-23): on the left, its views: **Development**, **Board**, **Modules**, **Workflows** and **Diagnostics** (on a phone, under **☰** at the top left, which names the page you're on). **Board** opens first, with **Is it delivering?**; **Development** is its own page from the left. At the top, **Alerts**, with the board's one alarm count (2026-11-24): every open trip and every warning not yet seen, the same number as the light in the top bar, the Board count and the alarm list's **Open**; beside it, each group's count, as "2 trips · 9 warnings · 2 caps". It opens to its groups, each with its count: Trips, Waiting for its run (trips started again, amber, until that run works), Warnings, Caps, Checks, Sync (Railway and the sync, when their watch needs a look), Seen (warnings someone has seen, listed but not counted: the system is still handling them) and Paused now (what's paused, counted on its own, never added to the alarms). A part whose trips were all started again reads **Waiting for its run** (amber), on its cards and its row, until that run works. Each item opens its part in the side panel. Notices aren't alarms to act on: they're in the alarm list under **Notices**. The alarm table under **Diagnostics → Alarms** says the same number for **Open**, and the queue's filter shows with the queue, under **Diagnostics → Observation and reporting**. **Paused now** has a page of its own, with what's paused, the core hosts, and the healthy, held and needs-me boxes. The six stages and the board's tables (Incidents, Alarms, Logbook, Ledger, Upkeep, Reports, Scheduled tasks) are under **Diagnostics**. Modules (the Service register) and Workflows open their own pages. When a link opens one part in the side panel (an alarm's service, a job, its register entry), the panel shows that part's card alone; **Open the full page** in its header has the whole list.
- **Three views first** (the control board's build): **Is it delivering?** (where it opens: three questions, what's healthy, what's held and what needs you, then a gauge card for each promise the site makes), **Development** (the Work queue first, then the 16 development gauges and Site health's list) and **Incidents** (every Incident Report, IR-####). The six stages follow as tabs after them. Each gauge card opens its snapshot; see "What are the gauges on the System Control Board?".
- **One control board in the menu** (2026-11-20): the board's own tables are its tabs, beside its stages: **Alarms**, **Logbook**, **Ledger**, **Upkeep**, **Reports** and **Scheduled tasks**. **Automation** (the master switch, Start the system, Pause everything, its workflows, tasks, services and changes) is in its **Start-up** stage, and the pre-flight's page (Run all, each line's Test, its runs and report) is in **Pre-flight** and **Start-up**. They're no longer separate entries in System Configuration's menu; their old addresses open the right tab, landing on the same spot (for example https://traversence.com/admin/config/scheduler#job-control_tick opens Scheduled tasks on that job). A page with its own forms loads in place when its tab first opens.
- **The lines at the top: Health now and Paused now** (2026-11-20): first one plain health line: the site's critical duties (page load, search, sign-in, payments, database, email and site jobs), then each part of the system with its counts, e.g. "Data gathering: 1 down (W1 Listings), 2 parts waiting on them, 2 off on purpose". It's red only when a critical duty is down. Under it, **Paused now** in three groups: **Needs attention** (each trip, with its fix), what **waits on** each trip folded under it, and **Off on purpose** (switched off by a release or a person, with what each waits for), folded away. Something the board switched off for a trip is the trip's, never "paused by a person": it names its trip. Each one opens where it's switched or fixed in the side panel: a workflow's page, a task's switch, a service's card. With nothing paused it says so. Beside it, the three core hosts (Bluehost, Railway and GitHub; if one is down, everything holds). The same lines are at the top of **Automation**.
- **The pre-flight's result is in the Start-up stage** (and Pre-flight's): its last result and its time ("Pre-flight, 10:19 AM: 15 held: W1, W2 and 13 more"). Each name opens its alarm, **Open the report** takes you to the pre-flight's lines and **Run it again** runs it in place; a result over an hour old says so. Once something is on, its checks are its **Diagnostics**, never its pre-flight: a service's card says Diagnostics once it's active, and Automation's **Check before switching on** reads **Diagnostics** once Start has run and the master switch is on.
- **On a phone** (2026-11-19): the board fits the screen at any width: nothing scrolls sideways, the header and account bar stay whole, and long addresses in an alarm or a part's "Where it stands" wrap onto the next line.
- **The six stages:** pre-flight, start-up, observation and reporting, shut-down, maintenance and history, with the one the system is in now lit and marked "now". They're tabs: each tile says in a line what its part is for, and pressing it shows only that part of the board (everything else goes away until you pick another): Pre-flight its lines; Start-up and Shut-down the controls and the systems; Observation and reporting the alarms, the systems and the query timings; Maintenance its windows and the systems; History the logbook and the reports. The stages open on the one the system is in (the board itself opens on Is it delivering?), and a link to a part (an alarm, Maintenance, the logbook) opens its tab. The line under them says where the system stands; running with alarms open says how many, and that their fixes are under Observation and reporting.
- **Every alarm leads to its own fix:** each one's buttons go to the one thing to fix, never a list to search: a server error opens the failing call in the Ledger; a failed, starved or silent crawler run opens that run; a failed task offers **Try it first on the next run** and **Pause the task** in place; a job that stopped, hung or ran slow opens its own scheduled task; a worker gone quiet links to Railway (a link only: nothing is restarted from the site) with **Check**; a pre-flight alarm carries its line's own fix (the setting, the switch or the company's page) and its line; a window that didn't come back opens Maintenance. Any link that names a part of a page opens it, unfolds what it's in and outlines it for a moment. A link that opens beside the board lands on the one thing, opened and in view: a service's alarm opens its card in the Service register, and a workflow's or the crawler's opens its run (the one it names, else the run going at the time) (2026-11-19).
- **Its own controls:** Run the pre-flight, Start the system, Shut down in order, and Pause everything, the emergency stop. Finding a part is the parts table's own search (below): by its name, what it needs, or one of its sources ("Overture" finds what reads from Overture Maps).
- **Alarms, the board's queue:** one row per problem, worst first (a pre-flight alarm's words follow its check as it stands now, refreshed every 5 minutes, so a trip names the part that holds it (a red part, else an amber one, never one that passes), or, once its check passes, closes as **Recovered**; a red alarm left from before trips had their time is tested again by the system and settled: closed if it passes, amber if it's amber today, still red only if it's red today, each noted in the logbook); a process with an open trip shows **Check and start again** on its row instead of Start, and its **Check** says the trip stays red until you press it (a check never clears a trip); and if the 10-minute job that takes releases from git hasn't looked in 30 minutes, a warning says so, with **Check for builds** beside it: what happened, what it means, what to do, its fix as a button, **Open its runs** (or its line, or its card) in a drawer, **Acknowledge** (and **Undo**), how many times and since when, and which process it holds. A long reading is one line with a ▸ toggle. An outside service's alarm has **Check it now**; a passing check closes it (there's no Mark resolved). One dropdown shows Open, Acknowledged, Cleared or a level. A problem raises its alarm the moment it happens (a worker's call that fails, a crawler run that fails, a service that refuses us, a red pre-flight line), or within 5 minutes when something goes quiet; the same problem again updates its alarm. A warning or notice clears by itself, saying how, when its reading is good again. The list opens by itself whenever anything is open. Alarms of the same kind in the same part of the system are one row ("5 engine feeds held", naming what they hold): it opens to each alarm with its own buttons, and **Acknowledge all** acknowledges every one. Pulse shows the same group as one item that opens to them.
- **Red and amber:** red is only for a trip (stopped, waiting for an admin) or a core host down (Bluehost, Railway, GitHub). Something held, deferred or timing out is amber, with a pause mark and the word Held or Holding. A part with an open warning shows **Warning**, amber with "!", never a green ✓ beside it. A workflow whose failing task is covered by a backup is **On a backup**, amber, naming the source answering meanwhile ("Businesses in bulk is answered by Ours"), never Holding. A system's line counts them apart: "4 tripped · 1 held". A warning has no **Acknowledge**: the system is handling it, and it clears itself or becomes a trip.
- **A source down holds only what uses it** (2026-11-21): when an outside service a workflow uses is down, the board checks its role. If what the workflow needs from it still has a source that works (its first, or a backup), nothing waits, and its line says **A source is down and covered**. If only some of the workflow's steps call it, only those steps wait (**Waiting while their source is down**), amber, and the rest runs; they start again by themselves once its line is green. The whole workflow waits only when every step calls that service. A service whose check reaches one of its servers but not another reads **Partly working**, naming the one step that waits. For example, Common Crawl is only W3's backup, so its being down never stops W3's own reading.
- **A trip from the crawler clears by itself** (2026-11-21): when the site's own check reaches the service again, the crawler's back-off there is closed too and its trip closes as **Recovered**. Never for a refusal, a key or a quota, and never while a service has asked us to wait.
- **Each alarm's first button opens what fixes it** (2026-11-20): an outside service's card, a pre-flight line, a workflow's or task's own page, or a site job on Scheduled tasks, in the side panel over the board. You never leave the board. A link inside the panel replaces what it shows, with **← Back** to where it was: there's only ever one panel, and ×, Esc or a click outside closes it. The same goes for the drawers in the admin's lists (2026-11-23): an alarm opened from the board's **Alarms** tab, then its part's own page, then its register entry, all show in that one drawer, each titled by the part's name ("Google Cloud"), with **← Back** to each step; on a phone it takes the whole screen and × or Esc closes it. The alarm's run, if it has one, stays beside it as **Open its run**.
- **A trip is what the system couldn't fix itself.** When something fails (a server error, a failed run, a job that stopped, a worker gone quiet), the system first tries again by itself, and the alarm is a **Warning** saying which try it's on. After 3 tries, or an hour, whichever comes first (both set in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**), it's a **Trip**. The hour counts even if the failure doesn't come again (a task that runs only at night, for instance): once it's up, the board's watch makes it a trip, saying its tries ran out (2026-11-20). A refusal or running out of budget is a trip at once: there's nothing to try. A trip is the system placing a pause (2026-11-20): it pauses what its fault touches, and only that: one workflow when its task keeps failing, one task, or one outside service out of budget; all gathering only when the crawler itself is down (runs that fail outright or never report). It never turns anything off: every switch stays as the person who set it left it, and the pause shows beside it, "On · Paused by the system (a trip): …", on Automation, on the service's card and on the board. A site job that isn't part of a workflow can't be paused from here, so it only trips. The row says what it paused. A slow call or job is never a trip: it's a warning that says how slow, stops nothing, and clears by itself when the speed returns. A call that brings in records is judged per record, so a big batch isn't called slow. A phase (Phase 1 to 6) raises no alarm of its own: it only adds up its workflows, whose own alarms say what holds them. When an outside service stops answering the site, the system checks it again by itself once its wait is over (hourly while nothing else calls it), and **Check it now** on its alarm checks it at once; an answer closes it. A site that refused or blocked us is never retried by the system: it waits for a person. When its check passes, a trip clears itself (2026-11-20), escalated or not: it closes as **Recovered** (a notice in the logbook and Pulse to whoever it was escalated to, nothing to press) and what it paused runs again. **Check and start again** is there for when you don't want to wait: it runs the check, then lifts the pause, with no question to answer; if the check is still red, the pause stays and the trip names the part that holds it. Switches a trip turned off before this change were put back to what a person last set, logged as "Put back to what … set on …". **Undo the Resolve** is there for a day.
- **Critical parts trip at once** (2026-11-19): "Critical is that our site can perform its duties": page load, search, payments, database connections and system-critical procedures (sign-in, releases, the scheduler, the board's watch, system email). A part carrying one of these duties is marked on its row ("Payments: critical"). Its first failure is a trip at once, to Pulse, email and push, while the system keeps trying. If the system fixes it, the trip closes itself and you get a notice, "Recovered: QuickBooks was down 4 min, the system fixed it", with nothing to press. If its tries run out, an **Escalation Notice** ("Retries unsuccessful, now admin required") follows, and only **Resolve** clears it. Which parts are critical, and the duty each carries, is set under **Critical parts** on https://traversence.com/admin/config/thresholds#critical-parts (each change is in the logbook). The crawler, W0 Indexer, the video worker and enrichment aren't critical by themselves: the site does its duties without them. **Search, Sign-in and Checkout and payments** are watched as parts of their own: a test search every 5 minutes (it records nothing: no demand, nothing sent to the crawler, no view), QuickBooks' connection (read on the site every 5 minutes, asked of QuickBooks at most once an hour), and visitors' server errors on their pages (the page's address and the error only: never what was searched, who, or from where; kept 7 days). Server errors on 3 or more requests in 5 minutes (changed in **Thresholds & gates**), or a failed test, is that part failing; fewer are kept on its row as a notice. QuickBooks refusing us or needing reconnecting trips at once.
- **The light in the admin's top bar:** on every module page, the count of open trips (seen or not, pressed and waiting for their run or not) and of warnings not yet seen, the same number as the board's **Alerts**; red only for a trip still holding, amber for warnings and for trips started again and waiting for their run; it opens the alarm list. A trip is emailed to the Founder at once (any hour) and pushed to the Founder's phone at the session manager's next hourly check. A warning is never emailed or pushed: the system clears it itself, or it becomes a trip, which reaches you at once.
- **In Pulse:** only trips, each its own Critical item: a trip is the one thing a person is told about at once; it closes itself as Recovered once its check passes, or with **Check and start again**. Warnings stay on the board, in its light and count in every admin page's top bar (not the Dashboard's, where Pulse lists the trips) and in the reports, because the system clears them itself or turns them into a trip (2026-11-19). A service near its monthly cap is a warning at 50%, 75% and 95% (on the board), and a trip only when it's out of budget; a task that keeps finding nothing is a warning of its own. Each trip carries its old case's buttons, done in place: **Raise the cap**, **Pause it**, **Check it now** and **Pause the task**. They're no longer separate Pulse cases, and each alarm's Pulse item has the same buttons as its row on the board. **Done** there acknowledges it; it goes by itself when the alarm clears. A trip's item goes to the Director's level, with **Resolve** (what fixed it) and, after, **Undo**. The last of the old cases are alarms now too: each thing **Starting the system** waits on (with its button), each of **W0 Indexer**'s needs (Reactivate, It's fine), and a **pre-flight run that stopped** (Continue, Cancel). A trip's email isn't a second row in your Pulse: the trip's own item (or its group's) is the one place it shows.
- **A row's last error and what it means:** when a worker's call to the site fails, its row says what went wrong in the site's own words, and when; a red row starts with why, from its latest runs (one failure in a day, with the latest fine, is amber), and says what it means for the system.
- **Query timings:** the four heaviest queries against their limits, with Re-test the four. A slow one is noted and holds nothing.
- **The parts of the system** (one table, the admin's standard, 2026-11-19): the four systems (the Knowledge Engine, Workers, Outside services and the site itself) are group rows; press one and it opens to its parts. Each part is a row with its status (a word with its colour), its name (its phases or what it is under it, and its duty when it's critical), its stage, where it stands in a few words, its last 24 hours (runs, and how many records came in and how much of that is accounted for), its speed per record and its OK rate (a column with no history yet says so once, under the table), **Check**, and **⋯** with words: Its own page, Check and start again, Start it, Shut it down, Take out for maintenance, Put back; one that isn't open to it is greyed and says why. A box searches the parts, and **Show** filters them by status (Tripped, Held, Off, Not tested…) with each one's count; on a phone each part is a card. Pressing a part's name opens its own page in a drawer, beside the board, with short labelled lines:
  - its route;
  - its check in a line: what holds it first, on one line with a ▸ toggle: press it (or the line) to read the rest in place, and again to fold it, and its other parts (each ✓ or ✗) on a toggle, never repeated. A line tested before checks were kept part by part says so: press Check to see which part holds it;
  - its **needs** as chips: each need with ✓ or ✗ and the source that answered it (its sources in order, ours first, when you point at it); press a chip to test that need;
  - its last 24 hours: what came in, and where every one went (stored, updated, sent to Review or refused);
  - the records it touched (each opens in a peek);
  - its controls: **Run its check**, **Shut it down** (its tasks first, then the workflow) and **Start it**, which starts it only when its check isn't red.
- **The site's own needs:** **What the site needs from outside**, a panel of its own under the table, lists them in the same way. None of them holds the engine. **Trends by system** (under History) draws each system's processes summed, over 24 hours, 7 or 30 days.
- **Maintenance:** a workflow, or a task with its own switch, can be taken out of service on purpose. **Take out for maintenance** under its row asks why and brings it back by itself after a set time (2 hours unless changed in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**); the **Maintenance** panel (https://traversence.com/admin/config/control#maintenance) sets any window, now or ahead: the process, when it goes out (empty for now), its **Back by** and why. While it's out it's shut down in order (its tasks, then the workflow), shows blue with the stage "In maintenance", its new alarms are notices that say so (a trip already open stays a trip until an admin resolves it), and Start the system skips it, saying why. It's never shown as a failure. **Put back** runs its check first and starts it again only if the check isn't red; a red check keeps it out with the reason on its row. A window set ahead goes out and comes back by itself (the board's 5-minute watch), and both are logged as notices; at its Back by, a red check keeps it out and raises a warning. A window that hasn't started can be cancelled. A window whose start the watch missed (the site or its jobs down) still goes out at the next watch and says how late; one whose Back by passed before any watch ran is logged as missed. Under the windows, the **upkeep calendar** lists what's due, read from what the system already schedules: the bulk and monthly loads in the crawler's night window (1 to 5 am Arizona), Data cleanup's daily pass, the checks due on held services, the nightly pre-flight and staging's nightly pass. Each opens in place: its process on the board, or its scheduled task in a drawer.
- **History and trends:** the board keeps each process's readings over time: its light (written when it changes, and at least once an hour) and each hour's counts (worker calls, records in, stored and refused, server errors, the time a call takes, runs and failed runs, and for an outside service this month's spend and its share of its cap). Open a process, or a whole system, and its **Trend** shows bars of its work over the last **24h**, **7d** or **30d** (press to switch) above a strip of its light (green working, amber held, red down, blue in maintenance), with its **uptime**: the time it was working against the time it should have been on (time switched off or in maintenance doesn't count against it). A **system's** strip turns an hour red only when a critical part (page load, search, payments, the database, system procedures) was down (2026-11-20); another part held or down that hour makes it amber, named under the strip ("Held: Common Crawl (3 hours)"). Each strip says what it counts under it. Until anything has been recorded it says "No history yet: readings start now". Every reading is kept 30 days (set in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**, https://traversence.com/admin/config/thresholds), and one row per reading per day for 400 days. Insights → Telemetry points here.
- **The logbook** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/control#log): every switch, control, alarm and note, newest first: alarms raised, acknowledged, cleared and resolved; shut-downs, starts and maintenance; each control pressed on the board; Start the system's sessions; the pre-flight's runs; outside services' issues opened and resolved; site jobs that stopped; and releases going live. Each line says who (a staff member, or the system) and why, at the time where it happened, labelled. Its one filter shows the whole system, one system or one process: its list is grouped by system, with the parts that have entries this week first (each with its count) and the quiet ones greyed, and a box at the top finds a part by name. **Show older** carries on. **Log an entry** adds your own note, on the system or a process.
- **Where it's reached from:** Automation's **Start the system** card and the Workflow board's **Check before switching on** each link to the board, which presses the same Start and holds the same lines, process by process.
- **Sites that blocked us** (W0, 2026-11-19): one quiet notice on W0's row, "2 sites blocked us", naming each site and why: no crawling allowed (its robots.txt), a firewall or bot check refused us, or the failure's number ("403 failure"). Each has **Reactivate** in place, and **Every blocked site** opens the list on W0's own page on the board (https://traversence.com/admin/config/control/wf-W0#cb-held), each site with **Reactivate** (since 2026-11-20; **Insights → Index**, https://traversence.com/admin/insights/index#held, keeps the list to read, with a link to W0's page for Reactivate). It's a notice, not a problem, unless the sites blocking us pass a share of the day's reads (5% unless changed in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**, https://traversence.com/admin/config/thresholds#gate-w0_block_share): then it's a warning. Never a trip.
- **Its data** (2026-11-19): each process's own page has a table of the records its calls touched, newest first: each record by its number (a listing, a Review item, a source), what happened to it (Stored, Updated, Sent to Review, Refused), the call and run that touched it, and when. Its **Show** button filters by what happened, with counts. Each row opens the record, or the call in the Ledger when the record has no page of its own. Record numbers and what happened only, never a page's text or anyone's details. W0's page also counts its index by state (to read, read, blocked, outside our region…), linked to **Insights → Index**. A Review item someone already handled says what happened to it (published, merged or rejected, with its note), who did it and when, with a link to its listing (2026-11-20).
- **Pulse** (2026-11-19) is a part on the board, under the site itself: each time someone opens Pulse, the time it took to gather its rows is kept (the time only, never who opened it), and its row says how long it usually takes (the middle of the last 20 opens). Past 2 seconds (set in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**, https://traversence.com/admin/config/thresholds#gate-control_pulse_ms) it's a warning on the board, "Pulse is slow to gather (2.4 s). The fix is the Pulse store", a communications build; never a trip. Its time is also among the **Query timings**.
- **Sources read outside our region** (W3): the board's warning counts the sources read regularly that look to be about other places (a country's web address, or places mostly outside Arizona and New Mexico; never a shortened link or an ending like .io used everywhere), recounted every hour and cleared when none are left. Its button, **Open the N sources**, opens them beside the board: each with why it's flagged, how often it's read and its last facts, with **Pause** and **Keep**; **Pause all shown** asks first and can be undone for a day; and **Which outside sources are welcome** takes rules (a website, an ending such as .gov, or a kind), so the warning counts only what no rule covers. Nothing is paused unless you or a rule says so. Shortened links (goo.gl, bit.ly…) are followed by the system to the site behind them, a few an hour, and judged like any other.
- **Cleared alarms leave the list:** an alarm that clears (its reading good again, Recovered, or Resolved) goes straight to the **logbook** under History; the alarm list holds only what's open. There's nothing to mark Seen.
- **Acknowledge** takes an alarm out of the open list at once (it's under **Acknowledged** in the filter) and it comes back only if it gets worse; inside a group, the acknowledged ones are hidden too. The count in the admin's top bar, the Alarms panel's line and the filter all count the same alarms, each one (a group counts every alarm in it): the open ones not acknowledged. The top bar leaves out notices. Picking a choice in the filter brings the list into view, showing only those; Trips, Warnings and Notices show the open ones of that level.
- **Filtering the parts:** the table's one **Show** button lists each status with its count ("Tripped 1", "Held 2", "Not tested 4"); pick one and only those parts show, **Every part** to see them all. Each system's line counts its parts by status too ("2 tripped · 2 held"): press a count and only those parts show, with that system opened; press it again for every part (2026-11-19). **Open every system** and **Close them** sit under the table, and the systems you've opened stay open while the board draws itself again. An alarm's **Holds:** link opens that part's own page beside the board.
- **Each process's own page** (for example https://traversence.com/admin/config/control/wf-W1): its name in the table (or **⋯ → Its own page**) opens it beside the board, with its controls, its open alarms and their buttons, its readings, route, trend and check, its logbook, and its report. And four tables in System Configuration, under the board: **Control Board: Alarms** (every alarm, open, acknowledged or cleared in the last 30 days, with Acknowledge and Check and start again in each one's drawer, greyed with the reason when your role can't), **Control Board: Logbook**, **Control Board: Ledger** (each call a worker made in the last 7 days, what it handed out, stored and refused, and the records it touched) and **Control Board: Upkeep** (what runs next, and the maintenance windows).
- **Reports** (https://traversence.com/admin/config/control#reports): for the whole system, one system or one process, over the last 24 hours or 7 days: uptime; what ran, with records in, stored and refused; slow calls, retries and crashes; spend against caps; every alarm raised, acknowledged and cleared, and how long each was open; shut-downs, maintenance and switches; and the trend. Each opens as a page that reads on a phone, with **Download CSV** (one table per section, the page's columns) and **Download the page** (one file that opens anywhere). They're made from what the site already keeps, so they cost nothing; nothing private is in them (who is a staff member or the system, and email addresses and phone numbers are taken out). The daily report at 5 am Arizona time carries the day's summary in System status with its link, and on Mondays the week's.
- **Shut down in order:** stops the tasks, then the workflows in reverse start-up order, then the master switch and the crawler's schedule. It reads "stopped, safe" once no run is still going.
- **It opens closed:** every panel and system group starts closed, each with a line saying what's in it (how many are held, which timings are over their limit); open what you need, or use a link in the alarm list.
- **It stays current:** the board draws itself again every 30 seconds. What you've opened stays open.

The Pre-flight page stays where it is, and its lines are also at the foot of the board, where the result line's **Open the report** and the Pre-flight tile take you.


### I'm on the Traversence team. Where do I switch a crawler task on or off?
On its workflow's own page on the System Control Board (2026-11-20, decisions/0175 §6), for example https://traversence.com/admin/config/control/wf-W1, under **Its steps and their switches**. Each step is one task: what it does, the outside services it uses and each one's backup, where it stands (on, off with why, who and when, or held by the master switch, its workflow or the system), and **Switch off** (it asks why, shown with it) or **Switch on**. It's the one switch: **Automation**'s Tasks now list one line per workflow with **Open W1**, a service's card links each step it serves to it (**Its switch**), and **Insights → Crawler**'s Switch on and off open it. Every press is kept with who and why in Automation's **Changes**.
### I'm on the Traversence team. Does the pre-flight run every 15 minutes?

No. It's a check before launch: every test is run once, read only and free, so you know what's ready before **Start the system**. Its scheduled task only looks every 15 minutes; it runs every test at 2 am Arizona time, within 15 minutes of a change to the site's settings (.env), and whenever someone presses **Run all** or **Run it again**. Its **Runs:** line says when it last looked and when its last full run was. While the system runs, the board's 5-minute watch keeps each alarm's words in step with its line.

The vector index (Supabase) catching up after Start is not a problem: its line says how many listings are left, how fast they're going and about when it'll be done. It's held only when its task is off after Start (**Switch its task on** opens its switch at https://traversence.com/admin/config/ai#ai-vector_index) or nothing has come off it for a day.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do listings get a map point when the site can't reach the Census?
The crawler's map points task asks the site to send a batch of street addresses to the U.S. Census geocoder, and places what it finds (only a missing point or one at its ZIP's middle; confidential listings never). Since 2026-11-20, when the site's call fails on its connection (reset or no answer, as Bluehost's network sometimes does), the Census has a backup: the crawler on Railway sends the same batch to the Census itself and hands the answers back, and the site places them by the same rules (each says it came by Railway). If the Census doesn't answer there either, the batch waits for the next run: nothing is lost or guessed.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What does Start the system check before it switches a workflow on?
Before it confirms each workflow or task, Start tests again what that one depends on, so it never trusts an old answer. The outside services and workers it needs are tested again when their pre-flight line is more than an hour old (set it in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**, "Start the system: a service line older than this is tested again"). Then every source it reads (the source map's needs, ours first) is tested every time. It also times the four heaviest queries once each Start. A slow one is noted on the card and holds nothing; a query that fails is a step on the card with its line on the Pre-flight page. Everything else about Start works as before: one that isn't ready is held with its fix, and the rest go on.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Does the site keep a record of what the crawler and the other workers send in?
Yes. Every call a worker makes to the site (W0 Indexer, the crawler and the video worker) leaves one line in the System Control Board's ledger. Each line records:

- what the call handed out or received;
- what was stored, what was updated, what went to Review and what was refused, with the reasons, such as "already known" or "kept what a person entered";
- the call's answer and how long it took;
- the crawler run it belonged to.

The listings and Review items each call touched are kept by their record number, so a run can show exactly what it brought in. The ledger keeps record numbers and counts only, never anyone's name, email or phone, or a page's text. Lines are kept 90 days and record numbers 30 days (both set in **System Configuration → Thresholds & gates**). Each run on **Insights → Crawler** (https://traversence.com/admin/insights/crawler) opens to its records: under **The last runs**, a run's link shows its page, with what each task did and its records by workflow, grouped as Added, Changed (which fields), Sent to Review and Refused (a field a person entered is kept, and it says so); each count narrows the list, and each record opens in a Peek. **What a workflow touched** picks a workflow and a day (today, yesterday or the last 7 days, in UTC) and lists its records, by kind and by run. Only what the ledger keeps is there: record numbers and field names, never the values before and after.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What are the Routes lines on the Pre-flight page?
Each process's route, from start to finish: the worker or scheduled job that carries it, the site's endpoints it calls, and the tables its answers land in. Each part is checked to be there. A missing endpoint or table shows red with what's missing; a worker that hasn't checked in yet shows as waiting. There is one line per workflow (W0 to W12, and W14), one per worker on Railway (W0 Indexer, the crawler, the video worker), and **Every endpoint the workers call is named by a process**, which turns red when an endpoint has no process watching it. For now these lines are for information and hold nothing. Coming: the **System Control Board** (System Configuration → System Control Board), which shows each process with what passes along its route, its alarms, its log and its reports.

### I'm on the Traversence team. Why does an alarm read the same on the board, in Pulse and on my phone?
Since 2026-11-20 (decisions/0176) an alarm is one thread, drawn in one shape wherever you see it:
- **Title:** what happened, in plain words.
- **Status:** where it stands now: "Warning since …", "Paused by the system (a trip) since …", "Escalation Notice …", or "Closed … Recovered", with its times labelled.
- **What it affects:** what the system paused, and for an outside service each need that uses it, with its backup or "no backup".
- **What the system is doing**, then **What to do** when a person has something to do, then its buttons.
- **Details ▾:** the raw evidence (status codes, the server's own words), folded.

The board shows all of it. Pulse shows everything but the details. A push shows only the title and status. The logbook keeps the finished story.

Since the control board's build the same words come from one place, the alarm as it stands now: an outside service's card (and Outside services, and Start) shows its issue with the alarm's own title and status, so nothing reads differently from the board. A part's plain name is used everywhere ("Lead scores (job)", "W2 Re-read websites"), never a code. When the system fixes it, the same message closes as **Recovered**; when a person's check fixes it, as **Fixed by** their name. A second trip with the same cause within 15 minutes joins the first one's Incident Report: its Pulse item is updated and the email goes as a reply in the same thread, with no new subject and no second push.

It's **one message**, updated in place, never sent twice. When a trip escalates, the same message is raised, so it's unread again and your phone alerts once more. When the system fixes it, the same message closes quietly as Recovered: a notice in your Pulse, nothing to press, no push.

- **An outside service's issue reads the same way** (2026-11-20, decisions/0176 release 2): on its card in the Service register, in its Pulse case, on its line under **Is it working** and on Start the system's card, one thread with the same title and status ("Common Crawl: its server failed at index.commoncrawl.org"; "Paused by the system (a trip) since …", "Backing off until …", or "Its wait ended: the next call tries it again"). The card shows all of it: what it affects (each need that uses the service, with its other sources or "no backup"), what the system is doing, what to do, its buttons (**Check it now**, or **Check and start again** on a trip, and **Its case in Pulse**) and **Details ▾** (what came back, how often and from whom, since when, its number). There's no × on it: it closes only when its check passes. The other lines link to it on the card with **Its issue**.
- **A trip's notice, on every channel** (2026-11-20): its email subject and its Pulse row read "SYSTEM ALARM NOTICE: <the part>: <what happened>", where what happened is **Paused by the system**, then **Retries unsuccessful, now admin required** if the system couldn't fix it, then **Recovered**. The push reads "SYSTEM ALARM: <the part>", its words starting with what happened. Each says what's going on, what it means, what to do, and links to it. One alarm is one thread: Pulse and the push update in place, and each later email replies in the first one's thread. A trip goes at once, whatever your quiet hours.
- **Fix it** (2026-11-20, decisions/0176 release 3): where a person can fix something, an alarm's first button is **Fix it**. It opens the one spot that fixes it in the side panel (its ×, Esc or a click outside closes it): for a refused key, the service card's **Its key**, with Replace ready to paste into; for a used-up quota or a cap, the card's **Caps and paces** (the community budget: **Costs**); for a site that blocked the indexer, its line under **Sites that blocked us** on W0's page on the board, with **Reactivate**; for an outside service that refused us, its issue on its card, with **Check it now** once they've lifted it. Fix it is the first button on the board's row, in the alarm's Pulse case and on the service's card. After you act there, its check runs on the spot: Replace runs Check (with **Put the old one back** if it fails), and setting a cap looks at the budget again and says **Fixed**, or what it still reads. When it's the service's own trouble (its server failing, not answering, or asking us to slow down) there's no Fix it: the thread says **The system is handling it**. On its card are **Check it now** (except while it's asked us to wait), **Pause it** and **Use backup**, which stays greyed until it's built. A push or an email about an alarm opens its Pulse case, with its Fix it already open beside it.
- **A step's Open in Pulse** (2026-11-20): a case opened in its row shows its tool in the side panel when you press Open. Before this, the press showed nothing.
### I'm on the Traversence team. What are the gauges on the System Control Board?
Each gauge is one arc from 0 to 100, with its number in the middle and its stage beside it. A gauge goes through five stages: **Planning**, **Phased Development**, **Audit & Review**, **Implementation** and **Monitoring**. Until it's watched, its score is how much of its setup is done (its probe built, its target set, its check passing, its alarm wired to the board), in the stage's colour. At Monitoring it's its reading against its target: green when met, amber below, red only for a trip, grey with "no reading yet" when nothing has been read. **Is it delivering?** has a gauge for each promise the site makes (pages load, search answers, sign-in works, email is delivered, trips reach a person…), grouped by promise. **Development** has the 16 development gauges; each stays in Planning until Jason approves its scope (**Approve the scope**, on its panel). Press a gauge to open its panel: what moves it, one bar of its items by where they stand (**Issues to fix**, **Not built yet**, **No check yet**, **Done**; press a part to see only those), **What you can do** (each card one sentence and one button), then its items, what needs you first. Every item, chip and part leads somewhere: an item's name opens its page beside the board, a check opens its result or what it found, a finding opens its evidence. A runtime gauge's panel shows its four setup steps in words, with **Approve the target** where one is proposed, and what it needs folded under one line. Three paths stay at the foot, **Workflows ▸**, **Core Services ▸** and **Registered Services ▸**. Every panel closes with ×, Esc or a click outside.

### I'm on the Traversence team. How do I fix something a gauge shows?
Open the item on its gauge's panel: it shows the issue and its evidence in place. Everything a gauge shows is fixed by building something, so its button says what it does: **Request a fix** (or **Request the build**, **Request the check**). The row then reads "Requested · waiting for a build to take it" with who and when, and **Undo**, which withdraws it at any age. Within the hour the request reaches the session manager, who hands it to the build session that owns it; the row then names that session ("Requested: the Control Board build session has it"), and the release once it's named ("Being fixed in …"). If no build has taken it after a day, the row says "Not picked up yet" and the session manager is alerted. You never mark it done: it clears by itself once its check passes again, and the gauge's counts drop with it. The item's other moves (its evidence, its page, its row in Site health, where it comes from) are under **⋯**. **Fix it** is kept for what the site can do on the spot, like an alarm's (2026-11-23).

### I'm on the Traversence team. What is the Work queue, and how do I add a task to it?
It's the first thing on the System Control Board's **Development** page (2026-11-24): everything we've found that needs doing, so nothing is forgotten and no gauge slips. Its gauge reads how many open tasks are on time (a task is **late** when it's a day old and nobody has it); until Jason approves its target (proposed: nothing late, with **Approve the target**) it shows how much of its setup is done, like every gauge. Under it, **Add a task**: choose the gauge or the area it belongs to (or **A new area…**), say what needs doing, why (what it fixes, or who asked) and where it came from (a decision, a hand-off, or whose words and when). The list shows what needs you first, late at the top, the first 10 then **Show all**; **Everything open ▾** filters it (Late, Open, Taken, Being fixed). Each task says where it stands: **Open** (nobody has it yet), **Late**, **Taken** (with whom), **Being fixed** in a named release, and it's **Done** by itself once its stages are through (below), or, for a gauge's **Request a fix**, once its check passes. Its one button is the next step (**Take it**, then **Name its release**); **✎** edits what it says, the old words kept in its **History** (the clock icon); **⋯** has the other moves: **Move to a session…**, **Name its release…** and **Withdraw, with a reason…**. Press a task's name to open its page beside the list (2026-11-24): its **Scope** (goal, in scope, out of scope, done when), its **Priority** (1 first, 2 next, 3 when there's room; open and late tasks are listed by it) and its **Steps**, each saying what to see, what to do and how it's confirmed, with **Confirm it's done**. Every field has **✎** to edit it, the old words kept in the task's **History**; a step confirmed by mistake is corrected with ✎ too. Under **Decisions**, the task names the decision records it rests on, each with its number and title; press one to read it on System Configuration → Decisions beside the board. **Attach a decision** finds one by its number or a word from its title, in the same list that page has; **✎** detaches one, kept in the task's History. The list shows each task's priority, its goal how many of its steps are confirmed and its decisions. Done and withdrawn tasks stay a week under **Done and withdrawn this week**. Since 2026-11-25 each task is one of three types, chosen under **Type** when you add it (Work if you don't choose). A **Work** ticket moves through four stages: **Planning**, **Building**, **Implementation** and **Confirmation**. It moves from Planning to Building with **Move to Building** once it has a goal, a Done when, a decision attached (or **No decision applies**) and someone who has it; until then the button is greyed and the line above it says what's missing. It moves on by itself once its release is named (Implementation), once that release is live (Confirmation), and once every step is confirmed (Done; a task with no steps is Done as soon as its release is live). **✎** beside the stages sends it back one or more stages, with your reason kept in its History. An **Audit** ticket is a finding from the daily check, looked at twice before it reaches anyone. A **Resolution** ticket is a decision only the Founder makes: its **Decision Brief** says the decision, why it needs the Founder, the line it crosses, options A and B with their pros and cons and which is recommended, the impact if it waits, and the cost. Each part has **✎** while it's a draft; **Send to the Founder** once it's whole. It then reads **Waiting for you** at the top of the queue and goes in the daily summary; the Founder answers with **Choose A**, **Choose B** or **Answer in my words**, on the task or in reply to the summary. A new task then records the answer as standing precedent, and the decision closes once that's done. The filter (one button) also shows each type and each stage, with counts, and the round people icon beside it opens the **Team**. Every hour the open tasks reach the session manager with the gauge's requests, and a late one is flagged to it.

### I'm on the Traversence team. What is the Team page?
System Configuration → **Team** (2026-11-25) lists the roles that work the Work queue. It has one row for each of the 34 roles in the plan, in four departments: Leadership, Operations, Build and Advocates. **Operators** move the day's work along. **Keepers** guard a rule or a record in the background. **Auditors** confirm work and run the daily check. **Advocates** are each bound to a test account (the Staff Views advocate to the staff test logins): they're a view for checks and never act. The **Founder** is the real login and answers the decisions only the Founder can make; the Founder is never an agent. Each department opens from its own row, and the roles on standby are folded into one group at the end. Press a role's name to open it beside the list.
